MANIFOLD STATE: {P,D} Unsubstantiated
  Chaitin's Ω is the halting probability of a universal prefix-free Turing machine.
  It is algorithmically random — its binary expansion is an algorithmic random sequence.
  It is non-computable — no algorithm can produce all its bits.
  The descriptor 'halting probability of U' is complete and finite (D is present).
  The value r = Ω is determined (P is present).
  But no T can extend the known bits — T is absent → {P,D} Unsubstantiated.

  The gap between the 64 known bits and the unknowable remainder is the
  Descriptor Gap at the value level: gap(Ω) = D_missing = 'the remaining bits'.
  The Descriptor Gap Principle says: this gap IS a Descriptor.
  And it is — it is the Descriptor 'algorithmically random', which describes
  precisely what cannot be further described.
